+# ADR-227: Narrow DCP-RED-MERGE-SEAM-0001 carve-out for the memory-spine identity files
+
+════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
+
+## Status
+
+* Accepted by the operator on 2026-10-31 (chat, verbatim "Accept ADR-227"; recorded in
+  `proof/MACRO-DMX-RUNNER-MEMORY-2A-001-R2/review_bundle/ADR227_ACCEPTANCE.md`). The text
+  accepted is this file at commit `95c54c4ec`; only this status entry and the front-matter
+  `status` changed on acceptance.
+* Drafted by `TP-DMX-RUNNERMEM2A-R2-W09-REDLANE-CARVEOUT-001` (workstream W09 of
+  `MACRO-DMX-RUNNER-MEMORY-2A-001-R2`). No workstream (W01, W02, W05) edited a red-lane path
+  before this acceptance (design section 5.6, packet invariant C12).
+* The operator chose this route on 2026-10-31 (`OPERATOR_RULINGS_R2.md`, ruling 1: "Narrow
+  carve-out ADR (Recommended)").
+
+## Date
+
+* 2026-10-31
+
+## Owners
+
+* Supervisor (DDD-Enterprises); drafted by the W09 implementer (Claude agent session) for
+  `MACRO-DMX-RUNNER-MEMORY-2A-001-R2`.
+
+────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+## Context
+
+`DCP-RED-MERGE-SEAM-0001` is a PreToolUse hard-deny red lane
+(`.claude/hooks/dcp_surface_guard.py`, backed by
+`src/dopemux/dcp/red_lane_rules.py::FORBIDDEN_PATHS`). Three of its blanket patterns cover
+the whole of:
+
+* `services/working-memory-assistant/` (dope-memory),
+* `docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/` (the ConPort server image source), and
+* `services/dopecon-bridge/`.
+
+PR Steward also classifies a PR that touches those trees as `dcp_boundary`.
+
+Runner Memory Parity Wave 2A (design `docs/91-rfc/rfc-2026-10-30-runner-memory-parity-2a.md`,
+revision 2) gives dope-memory and ConPort verified per-runner identity on HTTP writes
+(goals G1 and G2). The W00 go/no-go findings
+(`proof/MACRO-DMX-RUNNER-MEMORY-2A-001/review_bundle/W00_FINDINGS.md`) found that W01
+(dope-memory) and W02 (ConPort) are NO_GO for a Claude Code runner because every file they
+must change is inside the blanket patterns, and that the in-repo ConPort callers under
+`services/dopecon-bridge/` (W05) are in the same position. Packet invariant C07 makes that a
+stop, not something to route around.
+
+Precedent for relief is ADR-224 (two workflow files) and ADR-226 (dope-context eval
+harness and a short list of service files). Both use a negative lookahead anchored with
+`\Z` inside the existing deny pattern, and both keep `red_lane_scanner.py` content scanning
+fully active.
+
+Constraints:
+
+* The exemption must be file-exact. A directory exemption would let the whole tree be edited
+  and would need a `..` traversal companion rule (as ADR-226 needed for `eval/`).
+* Every other path in the three trees stays blocked, including same-named files in other
+  directories, suffix, prefix and case variants, and anything the allowlists do not name.
+* `services/task-orchestrator/` is a non-goal of Wave 2A and stays fully blocked.
+* The exemption is for the files Wave 2A needs; it is not a general lift of the seam.
+
+────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+## Decision
+
+Replace three blanket patterns in `FORBIDDEN_PATHS` with the same patterns plus file-exact
+negative lookaheads, each anchored with `\Z` and compiled with `re.DOTALL` (the ADR-224/226
+construction):
+
+```python
+re.compile(
+    r"^services/working-memory-assistant/"
+    r"(?!dope_memory_main\.py\Z)"
+    r"...  # nine files in total, listed below
+    r".*\Z",
+    re.DOTALL,
+)
+```
+
+The same shape applies to `^docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/` (nine files) and
+`^services/dopecon-bridge/` (two files).
+
+### The 20 exempted files
+
+**W01: dope-memory (`services/working-memory-assistant/`, 9 files)**
+
+| File | Why (design section; W00 evidence) |
+|---|---|
+| `services/working-memory-assistant/dope_memory_main.py` | Server entry: required ids on HTTP writes, workspace binding check, auth mode, health capability (5.1, 5.5). |
+| `services/working-memory-assistant/mcp_http.py` | The streamable HTTP surface and `POST /tools/*` write routes that carry the verified principal (5.1). The CI-run endpoint test pins this file. |
+| `services/working-memory-assistant/auth_identity.py` | New file: bearer verification against the hashed token directory (5.2). |
+| `services/working-memory-assistant/eventbus_consumer.py` | Ledger writer fed from Redis; records its self-asserted internal principal (5.3, operator ruling 3). |
+| `services/working-memory-assistant/chronicle/store.py` | SQLite ledger store: writes the principal column and applies migrations on first use (5.8; W00 design change 6). |
+| `services/working-memory-assistant/chronicle/schema.sql` | Canonical schema gains the nullable principal column (5.8). |
+| `services/working-memory-assistant/chronicle/sqlite_migrations.py` | Registers the new automatic migration (5.8; W00 design change 6). |
+| `services/working-memory-assistant/chronicle/migrations/v1_3_0_add_write_principal.sql` | New migration file: nullable `write_principal`, default `unverified` (5.8). |
+| `services/working-memory-assistant/tests/test_mcp_http_endpoint.py` | The one dope-memory test CI runs (`ci-complete.yml`, W00 design change 13); it must be updated and stay green. |
+
+**W02: ConPort (`docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/`, 9 files)**
+
+| File | Why |
+|---|---|
+| `docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/server.py` | REST and SSE surface: auth on every mutating route, the in-image SSE proxy forwards tokens, adds the streamable HTTP app beside `sse_app()` (5.1, 5.4; W00 design changes 8, 14). |
+| `docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/enhanced_server.py` | Enhanced tool surface that shares the write paths (5.1). |
+| `docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/instance_detector.py` | Instance binding and the `shared` instance value (5.1). |
+| `docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/conport_mcp_stdio.py` | The in-image stdio proxy forwards the caller's token to REST (5.3). |
+| `docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/auth_identity.py` | New file: bearer verification (5.2). |
+| `docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/schema.sql` | Canonical schema gains the principal column (5.8). |
+| `docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/migrations/008_write_principal.sql` | New migration (5.8). |
+| `docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/migrations/008_rollback.sql` | Its rollback: drop the column (5.8). |
+| `docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/migrations/conport_migration_gate.py` | Extend `REQUIRED_MIGRATIONS` if the gate requires it (5.8). |
+
+**W05: internal ConPort callers (`services/dopecon-bridge/`, 2 files)**
+
+| File | Why |
+|---|---|
+| `services/dopecon-bridge/dopecon_bridge/clients.py` | ConPort REST writers (`/api/decisions`, `/api/progress`, `/api/custom_data`, `/api/custom_data/claim`) must send their principal's token (5.3; W00 internal-caller list). |
+| `services/dopecon-bridge/ddg_mcp_stdio.py` | ConPort REST writers (`/api/instance/fork`, `/api/progress/promote`, `/api/progress/promote_all`) (5.3; W00 internal-caller list). |
+
+Some of these files do not exist yet (the `auth_identity.py` pair, the `v1_3_0` and `008`
+migrations); they are exempt by name so the workstream can create them.
+
+### Invariants
+
+* **No directory exemption.** Each lookahead names one file and ends in `\Z`, so there is
+  no directory exemption and no `..` companion rule is needed (unlike ADR-226's
+  `services/dope-context/eval/`). The existing traversal behaviour holds: a path spelled
+  with `..` inside the tree does not match a lookahead and stays blocked on its lexical
+  reading.
+* Every other path under `services/working-memory-assistant/`,
+  `docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/` and `services/dopecon-bridge/` stays hard-blocked,
+  including near-miss names (`mcp_http.py.bak`, `xmcp_http.py`), same-named files in other
+  directories (`services/dopecon-bridge/clients.py`, `.../sub/server.py`), case variants and
+  embedded or trailing newlines.
+* `services/task-orchestrator/` and `src/conport/` stay fully blocked. Of the 20 file names,
+  `auth_identity.py` and `server.py` recur across trees; each is exempt only at its exact
+  path.
+* `red_lane_scanner.py` content rules (`TEXT_RULES`: merge-seam calls, Dopetask execution,
+  network and subprocess calls, external-write endpoints, `LIVE_WRITE_READY`) are untouched
+  and still apply to every exempted file. This ADR is a path-level exemption only.
+* `tools/pr_steward/security_release_gate.py` imports `FORBIDDEN_PATHS` from
+  `red_lane_rules.py` and therefore follows this change automatically: the gate stops
+  classifying edits to exactly these 20 files as `dcp_boundary` and keeps classifying every
+  other path in the three trees. No change to the gate is made or needed.
+* `_FALLBACK_COMPILED` in `.claude/hooks/dcp_surface_guard.py` is unchanged; it never
+  covered these trees, so the fallback-subset-of-live invariant still holds.
+* The guard's control-character short-circuit and its case-folded and realpath candidates
+  are unchanged. The case-folded reading can only add denials, so it never widens this
+  exemption.
+
+### Non-goals
+
+* No content of any exempted file is changed by this ADR; the edits belong to W01, W02 and
+  W05 after acceptance.
+* No new exemption for any other file in these trees, for `services/task-orchestrator/`, or
+  for the sibling files that stay blocked (for example
+  `services/working-memory-assistant/conport_client.py`, which W00 found to be a mock and
+  which is excluded from Wave 2A). A new exemption needs its own ADR or an amendment here.
+* No change to `red_lane_scanner.py`, `dcp_surface_guard.py` or the PR Steward gate.
+
+────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+## Alternatives Considered
+
+**A. Run W01, W02 and W05 on a non-Claude runner with a recorded `dcp_boundary` approval.**
+Keeps the guard untouched, but leaves the seam as an obstacle for Claude Code on exactly
+the files the memory-parity work lives in, and splits one feature across runners. The
+operator chose the carve-out instead (ruling 1).
+
+**B. Exempt the directories or whole trees.** Rejected: it reopens the entire dope-memory,
+ConPort and bridge surface (including the unreviewed read and routing code) and needs a
+traversal companion rule for every directory.
+
+**C. A runtime allowlist file the guard reads.** Rejected on the ADR-224 grounds: a mutable
+authority surface for a hard-deny boundary.
+
+**D. Do nothing.** Wave 2A's memory-spine goals (G1, G2) cannot be met by Claude Code.
+
+────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+## Consequences
+
+* **Easier**: W01, W02 and W05 can edit exactly their named files through the normal Edit
+  and Write tools.
+* **Unchanged**: the blocked set shrinks by exactly 20 exact paths.
+* **Effectiveness depends on the session cwd.** The PreToolUse guard builds its repo
+  root from the hook process's current directory (`NativeHookAdapter.project_root`,
+  `src/dopemux/claude/native_hooks.py:275`) and loads `FORBIDDEN_PATHS` from the git
+  top-level of that directory (`.claude/hooks/run_native_hooks.sh`). A session whose cwd is
+  this worktree's root therefore uses the worktree's rules. The carve-out needs no change on
+  `main` for work done in that worktree.
+* **Failure modes introduced**: none identified. A lookahead that was written wrongly
+  would fail closed (extra denial) rather than open, and the tests pin both directions.
+* **Operator obligation**: accept this ADR before W01, W02 or W05 is dispatched.
+
+────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+## Migration Strategy
+
+1. W09 lands this ADR (status `proposed`), the `red_lane_rules.py` change and the tests
+   (`tests/test_dcp_surface_guard.py`, `tests/unit/runner_memory_2a_r2_w09/`).
+2. The operator accepts the ADR text (status becomes `accepted`, with a decision date).
+3. W01, W02 and W05 edit only the files listed above.
+4. When Wave 2A is complete the exemptions stay in force for maintenance of those files; an
+   operator may narrow or remove them with a later ADR.
+
+Rollback: `git revert` the W09 commit. That restores the three blanket patterns and removes
+the tests and this ADR. Nothing is pushed or deployed by this change.
+
+────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+## Verification
+
+* `uv run --frozen --extra test pytest -q tests/unit/runner_memory_2a_r2_w09/ tests/test_dcp_surface_guard.py`
+  (`tests/test_dcp_surface_guard.py` sits at the `tests/` root, which CI does not run, so it
+  is run explicitly).
+* Every exempted path is allowed for `Edit`, `Write` and `NotebookEdit`. Near-misses (same
+  name in another directory, suffix and prefix variants, case variants, embedded and
+  trailing newlines, trailing slashes at the regex layer, `..` segments, a symlink at an
+  exempt name) stay blocked. At least five other files in each of the three trees stay
+  blocked, and so does `services/task-orchestrator/`.
+
+## Notes
+
+* Related: ADR-224 and ADR-226 (precedents), ADR-MCPINT-002 (agent exposure and read plane;
+  amended separately by Wave 2A for the memory servers), design
+  `docs/91-rfc/rfc-2026-10-30-runner-memory-parity-2a.md` section 5.6 (revision 2).
+* The guard reduces a trailing slash with `pathlib.Path`, so `file.py/` reaches it as
+  `file.py`. A write to that spelling fails with `ENOTDIR`; the regex layer still treats the
+  slash variant as not exempt.
+
+────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+## Amendment 1 (2026-10-31)
+
+Status of the ADR stays `accepted`. This amendment adds two more file-exact exemptions, so the
+total goes from 20 to **22**.
+
+### The two added files
+
+| File | Why |
+|---|---|
+| `services/working-memory-assistant/Dockerfile.dope-memory` | The dope-memory image copies application modules one file at a time and does not copy the new `auth_identity.py`, which `dope_memory_main.py` and `mcp_http.py` import unguarded. A rebuilt image would fail at import. |
+| `docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/Dockerfile` | The ConPort image has the same defect: it copies modules one by one, and `server.py`, `enhanced_server.py` and `conport_mcp_stdio.py` import the new `auth_identity.py`. |
+
+Each file gets one `COPY` line for `auth_identity.py` and no other change. Both files are
+red-lane paths that this ADR did not exempt and that no R2 write surface listed (found by W02
+and W06, `W02_RETURN.md` and `W06_RETURN.md`, open item 1).
+
+### Authorization
+
+The operator authorized the extension on 2026-10-31 (chat, verbatim "Yes, extend 2A", answering
+"Extend 2A to fix the two Dockerfiles and the broken CI test?"). The record is
+`proof/MACRO-DMX-RUNNER-MEMORY-2A-001-R2/review_bundle/AMENDMENT_01.md`. The work is
+workstream W10 of `MACRO-DMX-RUNNER-MEMORY-2A-001-R2`.
+
+### Construction and boundaries
+
+* Same construction as the other 20: a negative lookahead per file, `(?!Dockerfile\.dope-memory\Z)`
+  inside the `^services/working-memory-assistant/` pattern and `(?!Dockerfile\Z)` inside the
+  `^docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/` pattern, both under the existing `\Z` anchor and
+  `re.DOTALL`. There is still no directory exemption and no `..` companion rule.
+* **The exemption is case-exact, at the regex layer.** `FORBIDDEN_PATHS` is read by PR
+  Steward's gate (`tools/pr_steward/security_release_gate.py`) and by `red_lane_scanner.py`,
+  both case-sensitively on git paths. They exempt exactly the two capitalized names above;
+  `dockerfile`, `dockerfile.dope-memory` and every other case variant stay blocked there.
+* **Claude Code's PreToolUse guard still blocks Edit-tool edits to these two files.**
+  `.claude/hooks/dcp_surface_guard.py` also tests the lower-case reading of every path
+  (ADR-226 residual F-001-A), and `dockerfile` / `dockerfile.dope-memory` match the deny
+  pattern, so a name with a capital letter never survives that reading. The other 20 names are
+  already lower-case. This fails closed and is accepted. A later change to the guard, not this
+  ADR, resolves it; no lower-case companion lookahead is added, because it would also exempt
+  those spellings from the PR gate and the scanner on a case-sensitive system, which the
+  operator did not authorize.
+* W10's two `COPY ... auth_identity.py` lines (one in each Dockerfile) were made under this
+  amendment, in a session where the guard did not fire for the worktree paths.
+* Every other Dockerfile name in those trees stays blocked: `services/working-memory-assistant/Dockerfile`
+  (no suffix), `Dockerfile.dope-memory.bak`, `xDockerfile.dope-memory`, `Dockerfile.dev`,
+  nested and case variants, and every Dockerfile under `services/dopecon-bridge/` and
+  `services/task-orchestrator/`.
+* `red_lane_scanner.py` content rules, `security_release_gate.py` and `dcp_surface_guard.py` are
+  unchanged.
+* Rollback: `git revert` the W10 commit restores the 20-file set.
+
+Verification: `tests/unit/runner_memory_2a_r2_w09/` and `tests/test_dcp_surface_guard.py` assert
+the 22-file set and the near-misses; `tests/unit/runner_memory_2a_r2_w10/` checks statically that
+each image copies the local modules its entry points import.

+---
+id: adr-mcpint-002-amendment-runner-identity
+title: 'ADR-MCPINT-002 Amendment: Verified Runner Identity on dope-memory and ConPort Replaces the Actor-Auth Precondition'

+prelude: Proposed amendment to ADR-MCPINT-002. For dope-memory and ConPort, verified per-runner transport identity on HTTP writes with identity-required writes replaces the actor-auth precondition. Codex is admitted while the servers run in log mode, with its writes verified. Enforce follows once the 2A blockers clear. The orchestrator precondition stays and moves to Wave 2C.
+status: proposed
+graph_metadata:
+  node_type: ADR

+---
+# ADR-MCPINT-002 Amendment: Verified Runner Identity Replaces the Actor-Auth Precondition for the Memory Servers
+
+════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
+
+## Status
+
+* **Proposed.** Not accepted. The accepted ADR file
+  (`docs/90-adr/adr-mcpint-002-agent-exposure-and-read-plane.md`) is not edited by this
+  document. Design 5.9 requires the operator to accept this amendment before the Codex memory
+  config ships (how-to step 8).
+* Drafted by `TP-DMX-RUNNERMEM2A-R2-W08-DOCS-ADR-001` (workstream W08 of
+  `MACRO-DMX-RUNNER-MEMORY-2A-001-R2`) from the committed W01 to W07 and W10 changes. The
+  operator chose this wording on 2026-11-01 ("Allow log mode (Recommended)",
+  `proof/MACRO-DMX-RUNNER-MEMORY-2A-001-R2/review_bundle/AMENDMENT_03.md`).
+
+## Date
+
+* 2026-11-01
+
+## Owners
+
+* Supervisor (DDD-Enterprises); drafted by the W08 implementer (Claude agent session).
+
+────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+## Context
+
+ADR-MCPINT-002, Decision 1, gives Codex the full MCP fleet only after two preconditions:
+
+1. DMX-MEMSPINE-IDENTITY-005: per-request workspace and instance identity, with fail-closed
+   writes on the memory spine.
+2. Orchestrator `actor_authentication.enabled`: every task-orchestrator transition and note
+   carries an authenticated actor.
+
+Its invariant is that no agent without enforced actor attribution holds a write-capable MCP
+config, and that write acceptance is enforced at runtime by the receiving service, not by
+config distribution.
+
+Wave 2A (`docs/91-rfc/rfc-2026-10-30-runner-memory-parity-2a.md`, revision 2) built a
+different runtime guard for the two memory servers:
+
+* Both servers fill an omitted `workspace_id`, and an omitted `instance_id` where the row
+  carries one, from their own binding, and reject an HTTP write whose `workspace_id` does not
+  match the server's binding (or any write to a server with no binding), in every auth mode
+  (W01, W02, W12; `services/working-memory-assistant/auth_identity.py:112-140`;
+  `AMENDMENT_04.md`). A write can therefore never be stored under another workspace.
+* Each server has an auth mode `off`, `log` or `enforce`. A write with a valid per-principal
+  bearer token is stored with its verified principal (for example `runner:codex`); any other
+  write is stored `unverified` and counted by caller. `enforce` rejects it with 401.
+* Tokens live in the macOS keychain; the servers hold only SHA-256 hashes (W03).
+* The stdio bridge sends the runner's token and fills the repo's ids (W04).
+* The generator writes Codex's memory config only when a gate holds (W07).
+
+The orchestrator is not part of Wave 2A. Upstream 4.0.0 is unreleased and moves to Wave 2C.
+
+────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+## Decision
+
+For **dope-memory and ConPort only**:
+
+1. **Replacement.** "Verified per-runner transport identity on HTTP writes, with
+   identity-required writes" (Wave 2A design goals G1 and G2) replaces the
+   `actor_authentication.enabled` precondition of ADR-MCPINT-002 Decision 1.
+2. **Codex is admitted in `log` mode.** The generator writes `.codex/config.toml` for the
+   memory servers when, for both servers, `/health` reports `auth_mode` of `log` or
+   `enforce` and `identity_required_writes` true (ConPort also `auth_principal_column`
+   `ok`), and a keychain token for `runner:codex` exists. `off` fails. In `log` mode every
+   Codex write is verified as `runner:codex`, because the bridge always sends that token;
+   writers without a token are still accepted and recorded `unverified`.
+3. **Enforce follows.** `enforce` is switched on per server after the 2A blockers below
+   clear, a smoke test passes, and the accepted-writer review is done.
+
+For the **task orchestrator**: the actor-auth precondition **stays in force and moves to
+Wave 2C**. Codex gets no orchestrator entry until then. The `full-sequenced` rows for the
+orchestrator (and for serena, dope-context, gpt-researcher and pal-stdio) stay in the
+preview only.
+
+### What stays exactly as in ADR-MCPINT-002
+
+* The DCP facade as the universal read plane, the exposure matrix rows for every other
+  agent, the loopback-only invariant, and the catalog as the single source for generated
+  configs.
+* Write acceptance is a runtime decision at the receiving service. The gate decides only
+  whether the generator writes a config.
+
+### Blockers for `enforce` (not available in 2A)
+
+From `W05_RETURN.md` open items 2 to 5, `AMENDMENT_02.md` and `AMENDMENT_03.md`:
+
+* Container callers (`adhd_engine`, `claude_brain`, `dopecon-bridge`) cannot reach the
+  keychain.
+* Container ids are unknown.
+* The bridge's progress route drops `instance_id` (red-lane file, not exempt).
+* Claude Code stays on direct HTTP until Wave 2B.
+* Other writers: `services/task-orchestrator`, `pm/adapters/conport.py`,
+  `services/dope-memory/mcp_stdio_adapter.py`.
+
+### Invariants
+
+* Tokens are keychain-only; servers keep hashes; no token enters a tracked file or a log.
+* A write is never labelled with a principal the server did not verify from a token, except
+  local writers that record a self-asserted `internal:*` principal with a marker (W06).
+* Servers bind to `127.0.0.1`.
+
+### Non-goals
+
+* The orchestrator, Wave 2B runner renderers, the installed-config doctor, and the Claude
+  Code move to the bridge.
+* Changing DMX-MEMSPINE-IDENTITY-005's own status. Whether the identity-required writes
+  built in 2A close that packet is UNKNOWN and is the operator's call.
+
+────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+## Alternatives Considered
+
+* **Keep both preconditions as written.** Rejected for the memory servers: the actor-auth
+  precondition is an orchestrator feature and says nothing about who writes to dope-memory
+  or ConPort.
+* **Gate Codex on `enforce` only (design 5.7 as first written).** Rejected by the operator
+  on 2026-11-01: `enforce` cannot be switched on in 2A, so Codex could never get a config.
+  Codex's own writes are verified either way; `enforce` only adds rejection of tokenless
+  writers.
+* **Ship Codex without a token check.** Rejected: that is the status quo the ADR was
+  written to prevent.
+
+## Consequences
+
+* Codex can write to both memory servers in 2A as `runner:codex`, and the stored principal
+  proves it.
+* Until `enforce`, any writer without a token still writes. The `log` period is the
+  window to find and fix or accept them. `dopemux mcp doctor` does not report them (not
+  built); the evidence is each server's `/health` (`auth_unverified_calls`).
+* A token holder can write as that principal. A compromised runner using its own token, a
+  second process of the same macOS user reading the keychain item or the ledger, and local
+  writers forging their self-asserted principal are not protected against
+  (design section 6).
+* The gate reads `/health` at generation time only. Switching `enforce` off later does not
+  withdraw an existing Codex config.
+* Claude Code's direct HTTP writes carry no token and, after the image rebuild, need the
+  bound ids in the tool call (W01 residual 1). This is a 2A limitation until Wave 2B.
+
+## Migration Strategy
+
+1. The operator runs the how-to steps in `docs/02-how-to/runner-memory-identity.md`.
+2. The operator accepts this amendment (status becomes `accepted`, with a decision date)
+   before step 8 writes the Codex config.
+3. Wave 2B moves Claude Code and the other runners; Wave 2C handles the orchestrator and
+   restates its precondition.
+
+Rollback: set this document's status back to `proposed` or `rejected`, restore
+`.codex/config.toml` (`git checkout -- .codex/config.toml`), and revoke
+`runner:codex` (`dopemux auth revoke runner:codex --apply`).
+
+## Verification
+
+* Gate tests: `tests/unit/runner_memory_2a_r2_w07/test_generator_gate.py` and
+  `tests/unit/test_mcp_commands_catalog.py` (W07 and its Amendment 03 round).
+* Live Codex acceptance: how-to step 10, `NOT_RUN` until the operator runs it.
+* Nothing in this amendment has been observed live.
+
+## Notes
+
+* Related: ADR-MCPINT-002 (amended), ADR-227 (the red-lane carve-out Wave 2A relied on),
+  design RFC `docs/91-rfc/rfc-2026-10-30-runner-memory-parity-2a.md` sections 5.5, 5.7 and
+  5.9, and `AMENDMENT_03.md` (the `log` mode ruling).
+* The `actor_authentication.enabled` wording remains in
+  `schemas/mcp/fleet-catalog.schema.json:148`, a schema description outside Wave 2A's
+  write surface (`W07_RETURN.md` residual 6).

+prelude: Revision 2 design for giving every agent runner verified, attributable access to the dopemux memory servers (dope-memory and ConPort), Codex first. Per-request identity on HTTP writes, one keychain-held bearer token per runner, a runner-neutral stdio route, and a narrow red-lane carve-out. The task orchestrator moves to a later wave.
+summary: Wave 2A revision 2 unlocks dope-memory and ConPort for Codex with verified identity on every HTTP write and moves Claude Code onto the same model. The task orchestrator (4.0.0 is unreleased upstream) moves to Wave 2C. Wave 2B (other runners' renderers, an installed-config doctor, the read-only facade, hook parity) follows.
+---
+# Runner Memory Parity, Wave 2A (revision 2)
+
+Status: **draft, for operator approval.** Nothing is built until this revision is approved. Revision 1 (sha256 `1fea6013…`, commit `77b4052f7`) was approved on 2026-10-31 and is superseded: its read-only investigation (W0) found facts that change the design, and the operator ruled on them the same day. Facts are from `origin/main` at `97b6d5040`, from this machine and from W0's findings (`W00_FINDINGS.md`, sha256 `4baa2184…`) unless a line says otherwise.
+
+## 0. What the operator decided
+
+Quoted from chat:
+
+- 2026-10-30. Build for every runner: "make sure we are building for all cli and desktop runners. claude code is the primary target, but codex is the main implementer and grok, agy, cmd code, and opencode (as well as copilot cli) get a fair bit of use too". Scope: "Yes, that scope". Identity strength: "Require verified identity". ADR change: "Amend it (Recommended)".
+- 2026-10-31, after W0 (`OPERATOR_RULINGS_R2.md`):
+  - DCP red lane: "Narrow carve-out ADR (Recommended)".
+  - Task orchestrator: "Split it out (Recommended)". Upstream has no 4.0.0 release (latest `v3.16.0`), and 4.0 removes every 3.x tool this repo calls.
+  - G2 scope: "HTTP writes only (Recommended)".
+- Revision 1's decisions about the orchestrator ("upgrade conport to 4.0.0" read as the orchestrator; "All at once" cutover) move with the orchestrator to Wave 2C and are not used here.
+
+## 1. Why
+
+Codex does most of the implementation and cannot reach the memory layer:
+
+- `ADR-MCPINT-002` gives Codex the full MCP fleet only after per-request memory identity with fail-closed writes (`DMX-MEMSPINE-IDENTITY-005`) and authenticated orchestrator actors. Neither exists.
+- dope-memory takes `workspace_id`/`instance_id` from process env with defaults (`dope_memory_main.py:57-58`, `mcp_http.py:115-116`). ConPort treats a missing `instance_id` as shared (`instance_detector.py:52-78`). The incident behind the ADR (writes filed under another repo's workspace) can still happen.
+- No ConPort or dope-memory route checks who is calling. ConPort's REST port (container 3004) is published on host loopback, and its SSE proxy forwards to REST with no credentials (`server.py:55-205`).
+
+## 2. Scope split
+
+- **Wave 2A (this design):** verified identity on HTTP writes to **dope-memory and ConPort**, the token tooling, the runner-neutral stdio route, a narrow red-lane carve-out, and generated config for **Codex** and **Claude Code** for those two servers. Acceptance: a live Codex session reads and writes both as `runner:codex`.
+- **Wave 2B (later):** renderers for Grok, AGY and Command Code; OpenCode and Copilot renderer fixes; the installed-config doctor; the DCP read-only facade; hook parity.
+- **Wave 2C (later, new):** the task orchestrator: upstream 4.0.0 once released, its token auth and strict config, the migration of the 3.x tool surface this repo calls (W0 counted 24 files under `.claude/` plus manifests, policy tables and tests), and the cross-repo `:7890` cutover (27 repo `.mcp.json`, 5 `.codex/config.toml`, 3 global configs). Until 2C, Codex gets no orchestrator entry.
+
+## 3. Goals
+
+| ID | Goal | Done when |
+|---|---|---|
+| G1 | Memory writes carry identity or fail. | dope-memory and ConPort reject an HTTP write without `workspace_id`, and without `instance_id` where the stored row carries one (5.1); tests prove it and run in CI. |
+| G2 | Every **HTTP** write to dope-memory and ConPort carries a verified principal. | Once a server is in `enforce` mode, an HTTP write without a valid token is rejected; the verified principal is stored on the write. Local writers that touch the ledger file or Redis directly are recorded as self-asserted internal principals (5.3). |
+| G3 | Runners get tokens without tokens entering a tracked file. | `dopemux auth issue <runner>` stores a token in the macOS keychain and writes its hash to the host hash directory; tracked files only reference it. |
+| G4 | Codex reaches dope-memory and ConPort through generated config. | `dopemux mcp generate --apply` writes Codex's entries only when the gate holds (5.7); a live Codex session reads and writes both as `runner:codex`. |
+| G5 | Claude Code keeps working on the same identity model. | Claude Code's config (produced by `dopemux mcp init`/`ensure`) uses the same route with `runner:claude`; its memory calls still work. |
+| G6 | The red-lane exception is explicit and minimal. | An ADR (status proposed until the operator accepts it) and a `red_lane_rules.py` change exempt exactly the files the workstreams name; every other path stays blocked; tests prove both. |
+| G7 | The ADR and agent docs match. | An ADR-MCPINT-002 amendment (status proposed) and agent-doc entries describe the new model, including that the orchestrator stays gated until 2C. |
+
+## 4. Non-goals
+
+- Everything in Wave 2B and Wave 2C. The orchestrator wrappers, config, hooks and tool surface are not changed.
+- The Python task-orchestrator service on `:8000` (it writes ConPort REST without credentials; see 5.5).
+- The full ConPort CRS v2 gateway.
+- Per-agent identity inside a runner; remote or multi-host deployment.
+- New instance columns on ConPort tables that have none today (5.1).
+
+## 5. Decisions
+
+Each is the supervisor's call unless section 0 quotes the operator.
+
+### 5.1 Identity model
+
+- **Principal = runner** (`runner:claude`, `runner:codex`; 2B adds the rest) **or internal caller** (`internal:hooks`, `internal:cli`, `internal:wma`, `internal:bridge-proxy`, `internal:dopecon`, `internal:adhd`, `internal:brain`). One token per principal, valid on both memory servers.
+- **Workspace and instance are request data, not identity.** `workspace_id` is required on every HTTP write. `instance_id` is required where the stored row carries one: dope-memory writes, and ConPort `workspace_contexts` and `progress_entries`. ConPort `decisions` keep `created_by_instance`; `custom_data` has no instance column (W0, `schema.sql:33-47, 109-118`) and none is added in 2A.
+- **Two sources must agree.** The stdio route fills `workspace_id`/`instance_id` from the caller's repo (its `.envrc.dopemux-mcp`); each server is bound to its workspace by its own env. A write whose `workspace_id` does not match the server's binding is rejected.
+- **Writes means every mutating route** (W0's list, larger than revision 1's): dope-memory's 10 MCP tools' write paths and `POST /tools/*`; ConPort `POST /api/decisions`, `/api/progress`, `/api/custom_data`, `/api/custom_data/claim`, `/api/context/{ws}`, `/api/instance/fork`, `/api/progress/promote`, `/api/progress/promote_all`, `PUT /api/progress/{id}`, `DELETE /api/custom_data`, and `POST /mcp` `tools/call` for every write tool.
+- **ConPort shared data** (supervisor's call): an `instance_id` of `shared` lands where `NULL` lands today; existing `NULL` rows read as `shared`; no row is rewritten.
+- **dope-memory stays one container per worktree.** Sidecar env (auth mode, hash-directory mount) comes from the generated compose override in `src/dopemux/mcp/docker_runtime.py:161-203`, not `compose.yml`; `generate_mcp_env` drops keys containing `TOKEN` or `PASSWORD`, so only the mode and the directory path travel through env.
+
+### 5.2 Tokens
+
+- `dopemux auth issue|rotate|revoke <principal>` and `list` (principals and dates, never tokens). Writes are dry-run by default and need `--apply`.
+- A token is 32 random bytes, base64url, in the macOS keychain (generic password, service `dopemux-mcp`, account = principal).
+- Servers hold only SHA-256 hashes, in one file under `~/.config/dopemux/auth/` (outside every repo, mode 0600 in a 0700 directory), mounted **as a directory**, read-only, into the containers (a single-file bind mount may not survive an atomic replace). The orchestrator's token format (YAML with `id`, `token_sha256`, `capabilities`) is 2C's.
+- Keychain reads must not prompt on every launch. W0 wrote the operator procedure to test the item ACL; it runs before W3 is dispatched, and its result sets the helper's ACL choice.
+
+### 5.3 Getting tokens to runners and internal callers
+
+- **The stdio route** (`src/dopemux/mcp/http_stdio_bridge.py`, extended): resolve the server's port from the caller repo's `.envrc.dopemux-mcp` (read only `*_PORT` keys with the existing `safe_port_int`; the file also exports a database password); read the principal's token from the keychain through the W3 helper; send `Authorization: Bearer` on every request; speak streamable HTTP (W0: `Accept: application/json, text/event-stream`, echo `mcp-session-id`, parse SSE-framed and plain JSON responses, print nothing for `notifications/initialized`); fail closed.
+- **A worktree without `.envrc.dopemux-mcp` fails closed** with an instruction to run `dopemux mcp init` there. Falling back to the main checkout's file would bind the worktree to the wrong containers.
+- Codex and Claude Code both use the stdio route for both memory servers.
+- **HTTP internal callers** send their own principal's token through the W3 helper (W0 counted about 15 ConPort REST writers plus `pm/adapters/dope_memory.py`). ConPort's own SSE proxy (`server.py`) and stdio proxy (`conport_mcp_stdio.py`) forward the caller's token to REST, or `internal:bridge-proxy`'s when the caller has none and the server is in `log` mode.
+- **Local writers are self-asserted** (operator ruling): `capture_client.py` (writes the SQLite ledger and applies its migrations), `native_hooks.py` (Redis `XADD`) and `eventbus_consumer.py` (ledger writes from Redis) record a principal such as `internal:hooks` with a `self_asserted` marker. They carry no token and are not part of G2.
+
+### 5.4 ConPort transport
+
+W0 confirmed revision 1's open branch: the REST `POST /mcp` surface answers `initialize` with `-32601`, lists 13 prefixed tools and lacks four. **ConPort adds `FastMCP.streamable_http_app()` (mcp 1.29) on the existing SSE listener beside `sse_app()`**, exposing the same 17 tool names Claude Code sees today. Authentication covers the REST port, the SSE surface and the new streamable surface; auth on the MCP surfaces alone would leave the REST data plane open.
+
+### 5.5 Rollout without cutting anyone off
+
+- Each memory server gets an auth mode: `off` (today), `log` (accept everything; store the verified principal or `unverified`; count unverified calls by caller), `enforce` (reject unverified HTTP writes with 401). Deploy in `log`; `dopemux mcp doctor` reports any server left in `log` and the unverified callers it has seen.
+- `enforce` is switched on per server only after a Claude Code and Codex smoke test through the stdio route passes, and after `log` mode shows no unverified writer the operator has not accepted.
+- **Known unverified writers that must be fixed or accepted before ConPort `enforce`:** the Python task-orchestrator service (`services/task-orchestrator`, red lane, non-goal) and anything W0 marked UNKNOWN (`pm/adapters/conport.py` posting to `/kg/custom_data`). The how-to lists them.
+
+### 5.6 Red-lane carve-out (operator ruling)
+
+`DCP-RED-MERGE-SEAM-0001` (`src/dopemux/dcp/red_lane_rules.py:84-86`, and `:44` for `services/dopecon-bridge/`) hard-blocks Claude Code edits under `services/working-memory-assistant/`, `docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/` and `services/dopecon-bridge/`, and PR Steward marks such PRs `dcp_boundary`. A new ADR in the ADR-224/226 pattern exempts **exactly the files named in the W1, W2 and W5 write allowlists**, as file-exact negative lookaheads (no directory exemption, so no traversal companion is needed). Every other path under those trees stays blocked; `red_lane_scanner.py` content rules still apply. The operator accepts the ADR before W1, W2 or W5 is dispatched.
+
+### 5.7 Generator gate
+
+`--allow-sequenced` is replaced by checks run before writing a write-capable Codex config for the memory servers:
+
+1. dope-memory and ConPort health report `enforce` and identity-required writes (capability fields added in W1 and W2);
+2. a token for the target runner exists in the keychain.
+
+If either fails, the generator writes the preview only and names the failed check. **Released:** dope-memory and ConPort through the stdio route. **Not released:** the task orchestrator (until 2C), serena, dope-context, gpt-researcher, pal-stdio. Gate text and tests that name `actor_authentication.enabled` (`mcp_commands.py:1100`, `fleet_catalog.py:306, 1195`, `tests/unit/test_mcp_commands_catalog.py:516`) are reworded. `mcp_catalog.yaml` and `src/dopemux/mcp/default_catalog.yaml` are byte-identical and change together.
+
+### 5.8 Schema changes and migrations
+
+- dope-memory's SQLite ledger migrates automatically on first use (`chronicle/store.py:103-116`, `sqlite_migrations.py:74`), by the server or by `capture_client.py` from any hook process. The principal column is added as a new file under `chronicle/migrations/` plus `chronicle/schema.sql`: nullable, default `unverified`. Whichever process touches the ledger first applies it; a nullable column keeps older readers working.
+- ConPort's principal column goes through `migrations/` and `conport_migration_gate.py` (extend `REQUIRED_MIGRATIONS` if the gate requires it) and runs only as an operator step, never on container start. The known migration-gate deadlock on a fresh database and its workaround (psql, then adopt) go in the how-to.
+- Each migration has a rollback (drop the column; rows written in `log` mode keep their data).
+
+### 5.9 ADR
+
+ADR-MCPINT-002 is amended (operator decision): for the memory servers, "verified per-runner transport identity on HTTP writes, with identity-required writes" replaces the actor-auth precondition, restated as G1 plus G2. The orchestrator precondition stays in force and is re-expressed in Wave 2C. The operator accepts the amendment before the Codex config ships.
+
+## 6. Security
+
+- **Protects against:** one runner writing as another over HTTP; writes filed under the wrong workspace or instance; an untokened HTTP writer once a server is in `enforce`.
+- **Does not protect against:** a compromised runner using its own token; another process of the same macOS user reading the keychain item or the ledger file; local writers forging their self-asserted principal; a hostile host process.
+- Tokens: keychain only; hashes on servers; constant-time comparison; never logged; rotation and revocation by command.
+- Servers stay on `127.0.0.1`. Anything left in `log` is visible in `dopemux mcp doctor`.
+
+## 7. Workstreams (proposed for R2)
+
+| ID | Work | Writes (indicative) |
+|---|---|---|
+| W0 | Done in R1; its findings are evidence for R2 and are not re-run. | — |
+| W1 | dope-memory: required ids on HTTP writes, binding check, auth modes, principal column, health capability; the CI-run endpoint test updated; `eventbus_consumer.py` records its self-asserted principal. | the W1 file list in the carve-out ADR; `src/dopemux/mcp/docker_runtime.py`; tests |
+| W2 | ConPort: required ids where rows carry them, binding check, auth on REST, SSE and the new streamable surface, `shared`, principal column and migration, the two in-image proxies forward tokens. | the W2 file list in the carve-out ADR; `src/dopemux/mcp/docker_runtime.py`; tests |
+| W3 | `dopemux auth` and the shared keychain/hash helper. | `src/dopemux/commands/auth_commands.py`, `src/dopemux/auth/`, `src/dopemux/cli.py`, tests |
+| W4 | Stdio bridge per 5.3. | `src/dopemux/mcp/http_stdio_bridge.py`, tests |
+| W5 | ConPort and dope-memory HTTP internal callers send their principal's token (the ~15 W0 listed, including the two `services/dopecon-bridge/` files under the carve-out). | the files W0 listed; tests |
+| W6 | Local writers record self-asserted principals (`capture_client.py`, `native_hooks.py`). | those two files; tests |
+| W7 | Generator: Codex config for the two memory servers via the stdio route, the real gate (5.7), catalog and Claude Code `init`/`ensure` rendering. | `src/dopemux/mcp/fleet_catalog.py`, `src/dopemux/commands/mcp_commands.py`, `mcp_catalog.yaml`, `src/dopemux/mcp/default_catalog.yaml`, tests |
+| W8 | Docs: how-to (operator steps in order, each with its rollback), agent docs, ADR-MCPINT-002 amendment draft. | `docs/`, `AGENTS.md`, `.claude/claude.md` |
+| W9 | The red-lane carve-out: ADR draft (status proposed), `red_lane_rules.py` file-exact exemptions, guard tests. | `docs/90-adr/`, `src/dopemux/dcp/red_lane_rules.py`, `tests/test_dcp_surface_guard.py` |
+
+Order: W9 first, then the operator accepts its ADR. W3 and W4 do not touch red-lane paths and may run before that acceptance. W1 and W2 follow acceptance; W5 needs W1, W2 and W3; W6 needs W1; W7 needs W1-W4; W8 is last.
+
+## 8. Constraints
+
+- Governance: one MacroPacket revision (R2), one final independent audit after freeze. Lane L3 (red-lane rules, compose override, catalog, hooks).
+- New tests go under `tests/unit/`. CI also runs `services/working-memory-assistant/tests/test_mcp_http_endpoint.py` (`ci-complete.yml:195-199`); W1 updates it. Tests at the `tests/` root (`test_dcp_surface_guard.py`, `test_mcp_config_generation.py`) are not run by CI and must also be run explicitly.
+- CI runs Python 3.12.13. Foundations Wave 1's port-drift test (PR #1485) must stay green.
+- Merge order: after #1482, #1483, #1485 and #1484.
+- Writes to the user's home directory (keychain, `~/.config/dopemux/auth/`, global runner configs) happen only through explicit operator-run commands with a dry run first.

+## Enforcement note for the R2 dispatch route
+
+W09 reported `carveout_effective_in_worktree = YES` for a session whose working directory is the worktree root: the PreToolUse guard builds its root from the hook process's working directory (`src/dopemux/claude/native_hooks.py:275`).
+
+This team-lead session's working directory is the main checkout (`/Users/hue/code/dopemux-mvp`). From there a worktree edit resolves to `.worktrees/runner-memory-2a/services/...`, which no `^`-anchored red-lane pattern matches (`.claude/hooks/dcp_surface_guard.py:85-104`). So for children dispatched from this session the guard neither allows nor denies on the carve-out: it does not fire at all. This is the known fail-open defect, reported separately and outside R2's scope.

+# MACRO-DMX-RUNNER-MEMORY-2A-001-R2 — Amendment 02: Claude Code stays on direct HTTP in 2A; suite-wide keychain guard
+
+**Recorded:** 2026-11-01 (UTC), by the team lead (Control Tower supervisor session), after W07 returned NEEDS_SUPERVISOR.
+
+## Ruling 1: Claude Code moves to the bridge in Wave 2B, not 2A
+
+**Operator words (verbatim):** "Later, with 2B (Recommended)"
+
+**In answer to (verbatim):** "When should Claude Code itself move onto the token bridge for ConPort and dope-memory?" The answer option read: "The tracked .mcp.json stays on direct HTTP; W07 keeps the bridge rendering available but not default. Codex still gets its gated bridge config now. Avoids Claude losing memory until a token is issued, and avoids Command Code, Grok and Copilot writing as runner:claude through the shared file. Enforce waits anyway for the container callers."
+
+**Why it came up:**
+- `.mcp.json` is tracked in git; the earlier research pass that called it untracked was wrong.
+- W07 rendered Claude Code's ConPort and dope-memory entries through the bridge as `runner:claude`. That fails four parity and repair tests unless the tracked file is regenerated.
+- Regenerating it would switch every Claude Code session in this repo to the bridge at merge time:
+  - The bridge fails closed without a token, so Claude would lose both servers until `runner:claude` is issued.
+  - Command Code, Grok and Copilot, which also read `.mcp.json`, would write as `runner:claude` (the Wave 2B design's shared-file finding).
+
+**Effect on the design (2A revision 2):**
+- G5 ("Claude Code keeps working on the same identity model") is deferred to Wave 2B, where the principal comes from the launching runner's environment marker instead of the shared file.
+- In 2A, Claude Code keeps its direct HTTP entries: unverified in `log` mode, and rejected in `enforce`.
+- So `enforce` cannot be switched on before 2B moves Claude Code. `enforce` was already blocked by the container callers (`W05_RETURN.md`).
+- G4 (Codex through the gated bridge config) is unchanged.
+
+**W07 correction:**
+- The default Claude Code rendering (`_render_local_entry`, `_build_local_mcp_json`, and through them `init`, `ensure`, `add`, `repair-config`) stays as it was.
+- The tracked `.mcp.json` is not changed.
+- The bridge rendering for Claude Code is kept as a tested function that nothing calls by default, for 2B.
+
+## Ruling 2: suite-wide keychain guard
+
+**Operator words (verbatim):** "Yes, add the guard (Recommended)"
+
+**In answer to (verbatim):** "Add an autouse test guard in tests/conftest.py so no test ever reads the real keychain (needed before you issue any token)?"
+
+**Scope added to W07:** `tests/conftest.py`, for one autouse fixture only. It sets `dopemux.auth.keychain.SECURITY_BIN` to a missing path, as W05's `keychain_guard_plugin.py` does, so every `get_token` raises `KeychainError` unless a test installs its own stub. W03's tests set their own stub after it, so they keep working.
+
+**Residual:** tests outside `tests/` (for example `services/*/tests`) are not covered by `tests/conftest.py`.
+
+## Still not authorized
+
+Push or PR; signing; the audit route; token issuance; migrations against a real database; the enforce switch; the ADR-MCPINT-002 amendment; the live Codex run; merge.

+# MACRO-DMX-RUNNER-MEMORY-2A-001-R2 — Amendment 03: the Codex gate accepts `log` mode
+
+**Recorded:** 2026-11-01 (UTC), by the team lead (Control Tower supervisor session), after W07 was committed (`29203f1eb`).
+
+**Operator words (verbatim):** "Allow log mode (Recommended)"
+
+**In answer to (verbatim):** "The Codex config gate requires both memory servers in `enforce`, which can't happen in 2A (container callers, and Claude until 2B). How should Codex get its memory access?"
+
+The chosen option read: "Gate check 1 accepts `log` or `enforce` (identity-required writes still true). Codex goes live in 2A and every Codex write is verified as runner:codex; tokenless writers stay accepted-but-recorded until enforce. Needs a small W07 follow-up and the ADR-MCPINT-002 amendment worded to match."
+
+## Why
+
+- Design 5.7 and W07 B01 make the Codex config write wait until both servers report `enforce`.
+- `enforce` cannot be switched on in 2A:
+  - container callers cannot present a token (`W05_RETURN.md`, open item 2);
+  - since Amendment 02, Claude Code's direct HTTP writes carry no token until Wave 2B.
+- So, as built, the gate could never write Codex's config in 2A, and G4 ("Codex reaches dope-memory and ConPort through generated config") could not be met.
+- Codex's own writes are verified either way: the bridge always sends `runner:codex`'s token, and the servers store the verified principal in `log` mode too. `enforce` only adds rejection of writers that send no token.
+
+## Effect
+
+**Gate check 1** now passes when, for both servers:
+- `auth_mode` is `log` or `enforce`;
+- `identity_required_writes` is `True`;
+- ConPort `auth_principal_column` is `ok`.
+
+`off` fails.
+
+**In `log` mode** the apply output states it: Codex's writes are verified as `runner:codex`, and writers without a token are still accepted and recorded `unverified` until `enforce`.
+
+**Gate check 2** (a keychain token for the runner) is unchanged.
+
+**Design deviation (2A revision 2):**
+- 5.7 check 1 is relaxed from "`enforce`" to "`log` or `enforce`".
+- G4 becomes reachable in 2A.
+- G2 ("once a server is in `enforce` mode, an HTTP write without a valid token is rejected") is unchanged: it was always conditional on `enforce`.
+- 5.5's rule that `enforce` follows the smoke test and the accepted-writer review stands, now also after container token delivery and Claude Code's 2B move.
+
+**W08:** the ADR-MCPINT-002 amendment draft says, for the memory servers, that verified per-runner identity on HTTP writes with identity-required writes replaces the actor-auth precondition. It also says Codex is admitted while servers run in `log` mode, with `enforce` to follow the conditions above.
+
+**W07 follow-up round:** W07's write surface (no new files) is authorized under this ruling. A03 of W07 permits an extra attempt only with a new operator ruling; this is that ruling.
+
+## Still not authorized
+
+Push or PR; signing; the audit route; token issuance; migrations against a real database; the enforce switch; the ADR-MCPINT-002 amendment's acceptance; the live Codex run; merge.

+# MACRO-DMX-RUNNER-MEMORY-2A-001-R2 — Amendment 04: omitted write ids default to the server's binding (W12)
+
+**Recorded:** 2026-11-01 (UTC), by the team lead (Control Tower supervisor session), after W08 (`05c1def3e`).
+
+**Operator words (verbatim):** "Default to the binding (Recommended)"
+
+**In answer to (verbatim):** "Claude Code (on direct HTTP until 2B) must now send each server's exact workspace id on every write, which it can't know, so its memory writes would mostly fail once the new images run. How should 2A handle it?"
+
+The chosen option read: "Each server fills an omitted workspace_id (and instance_id) from its own binding; a supplied workspace that doesn't match is still rejected, so nothing can be filed under another workspace. Claude's writes keep working. Small W12 change to the already-exempt dope-memory/ConPort server files plus docs; relaxes design G1 from 'reject a write without workspace_id' to 'never store a write under any workspace but the server's own'."
+
+## Why
+
+W01 and W02 reject any HTTP write that omits `workspace_id` (and `instance_id` where the row has one), in every auth mode. The values differ per server (`lifecycle.py:1218-1228`):
+
+| Server | Binds to | Example |
+|---|---|---|
+| dope-memory | `Path(worktree_root).name` | `dopemux-mvp` |
+| ConPort | `identity.workspace_id` or `worktree_root` | the full path |
+
+Instance ids are a worktree hash. W08 flagged the problem. Under Amendment 02, Claude Code stays on direct HTTP until 2B and gets no help filling these values, so once the new images run its writes would succeed only when the model guessed them right. The team lead missed this when recommending Amendment 02.
+
+## Effect
+
+**Omitted ids are filled from the server's own binding.**
+
+| Server | Omitted `workspace_id` | Omitted `instance_id` |
+|---|---|---|
+| dope-memory | its binding | `DOPE_MEMORY_INSTANCE_ID`, else `A` |
+| ConPort | its binding | `DOPEMUX_INSTANCE_ID` where the table has an instance column, else `shared` (NULL) |
+
+Empty strings count as omitted. A caller's value is never replaced.
+
+**Still rejected:**
+- a supplied `workspace_id` that differs from the binding;
+- a ConPort path workspace that disagrees with the body workspace;
+- every write to a server with no binding.
+
+**Schemas:** MCP write tools and REST request models mark the ids optional. The health field `identity_required_writes` stays `true`, now meaning "writes are bound to this server's workspace". W07's gate reads it, and its name is kept.
+
+**Design deviation (2A revision 2):** G1 changes:
+
+| | Wording |
+|---|---|
+| Before | "reject an HTTP write without `workspace_id`" |
+| After | "never store an HTTP write under any workspace but the server's own; reject a mismatched one" |
+
+The incident behind ADR-MCPINT-002 (writes filed under another repo's workspace) stays impossible, because the binding check is unchanged and the old env defaults (`default`) are not restored. G2 (verified principal on HTTP writes; tokenless writes rejected in `enforce`) is unchanged.
+
+**Docs:** W08's docs (`05c1def3e`) are corrected in W12 (B05). That removes the "writes without ids are rejected" claims and the caveat that Claude Code's direct writes may get 400s.
+
+## W12
+
+| Field | Value |
+|---|---|
+| Packet | `task-packets/MACRO-DMX-RUNNER-MEMORY-2A-001-R2/WORKSTREAMS/W12/TASK_PACKET.json` (copy: `W12_TASK_PACKET.json`) |
+| Packet sha256 | `5d021f2a03691aec03a6dcbd264a5bedede7c1faf94d7bdafe10a8099f5d2f55` |
+| Schema | `task_packet.v2` PASS |
+| Depends on | W01, W02, W07, W08 |
+| Lane | L3 |
+| Red-lane files | Every one it touches is already among ADR-227's 22 exemptions |
+| Route | claude-code-agent, `claude-sonnet-5-5` |
+| Caps | 90 min, 1.0M tokens, 200 tool uses |
+
+## Addendum: W12 write surface extended (team lead, within this ruling)
+
+W12 returned NEEDS_SUPERVISOR. `docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/conport_mcp_stdio.py`, ConPort's stdio MCP surface, still declares the ids required on its write tools, and the packet's write surface left it out. That was the team lead's omission when writing the packet.
+
+The ruling covers "the already-exempt dope-memory/ConPort server files", and the file is one of ADR-227's nine exempt ConPort files. So W12's write surface is extended by that one file, under this amendment, for B03 ("every MCP write tool marks workspace_id and instance_id optional"). Its tool signatures get the same treatment as `server.py`, with a schema test.
+
+W12 uses its one correction round for this. No other path is added.
+
+## Still not authorized
+
+Push or PR; signing; the audit route; token issuance; migrations against a real database; the enforce switch; the ADR-MCPINT-002 amendment's acceptance; image rebuilds; the live Codex run; merge.

+# MACRO-DMX-RUNNER-MEMORY-2A-001-R2 — Amendment 05: `make test` regression repair (W13) and the J04 test gate
+
+**Recorded:** 2026-11-04 (UTC), by the team lead (Control Tower supervisor session), after the freeze of `4550f3ff5` was held.
+
+**Operator words (verbatim):** "Yes, approve it"
+
+**In answer to (verbatim):** "Approve Amendment 05?"
+
+The message put to the operator listed three items:
+1. Commit the test fix as W13: 10 test files, no production code, including `tests/mcp/test_conport_surface_contract.py`, which is outside every write surface. The team lead makes it, because the macro's correction rounds are used up.
+2. Redefine J04's `make test` gate as "no new failures against main".
+3. Leave one known failure in place, `test_live_catalog_files_are_untouched_by_this_packet`, and disclose it to the auditors.
+
+## Why
+
+The operator approved freezing `4550f3ff5` ("Yes, freeze it"). J04-FREEZE requires `make test`, which had not been run, so the team lead ran it before freezing. It failed, and 2A caused the failure. The freeze was held.
+
+CI did not catch this: no workflow runs `tests/mcp`, and CI's unit job runs selected paths. The team lead's 2,127-test set did not catch it either, because it was a curated subset run in the services environment.
+
+| Cause | Origin | Effect under `make test` |
+|---|---|---|
+| The keychain guard in `tests/conftest.py` patched by dotted string. Four older test modules run `sys.modules.pop("dopemux", None)` at collection, so the walk from the new parent cannot find `auth` | W07 / Amendment 02 | every test errored at setup |
+| W05's `conftest.py` and `keychain_guard_plugin.py` used the same string paths | W05 | W05 suites errored at setup |
+| `conport_mcp_stdio.py` imports `Context`; the fake `mcp.server.fastmcp` in `tests/mcp/test_conport_surface_contract.py` had only `FastMCP` | W02 | collection aborted, so nothing ran |
+| That test passed two arguments to a fake of `_post_json`, which now takes three, and called the tools positionally after W12 reordered their parameters | W02, W12 | 4 tests failed once collection worked |
+| That test's fakes stayed in `sys.modules`, so other suites' `pytest.importorskip("mcp.server.fastmcp")` succeeded against them | pre-existing fake, exposed by W02's tests | tests that should skip ran and failed |
+| dope-memory and ConPort each ship a top-level `auth_identity.py`. W01 and W12 imported it by bare name, and older tests leave ConPort's directory on `sys.path` | W01, W02, W12 | dope-memory tests got ConPort's module |
+
+The two `auth_identity` modules never share a process in production: each service has its own image. This is a test-process collision, not a server defect.
+
+## Effect
+
+**W13 (test code only; no file under `src/`, `services/` or `docker/` changes):**
+
+| Path | Change |
+|---|---|
+| `tests/conftest.py` | guard patches the module object |
+| `tests/unit/runner_memory_2a_r2_w05/conftest.py`, `keychain_guard_plugin.py` | patch module objects |
+| `tests/unit/runner_memory_2a_r2_w01/helpers.py` | `load_dope_memory_auth_identity()` loads by file location; `pin_dope_memory_modules()` for one test |
+| `tests/unit/runner_memory_2a_r2_w01/conftest.py` | autouse pin |

+# MACRO-DMX-RUNNER-MEMORY-2A-001-R2 — Keychain ACL result (W03 precondition, B01)
+
+**Procedure:** `proof/MACRO-DMX-RUNNER-MEMORY-2A-001/review_bundle/W00_FINDINGS.md` section 6, run in part on 2026-10-31 (local) / 2026-11-01 (UTC).
+**Recorded by:** the team lead (Control Tower supervisor session), from the operator's terminal output and one read the operator approved ("yyeess").
+**Item:** throwaway generic password, service `dopemux-mcp-probe`, account `probe`. It is not a real token.
+
+## Step 1: create
+
+The operator ran `security add-generic-password -s dopemux-mcp-probe -a probe -U -w` in the Claude desktop app's terminal pane. It prompted for the value and asked to retype it, and printed no error.
+
+This is the **default ACL**: no `-T` and no `-A`, so the creating `/usr/bin/security` binary is the trusted application.
+
+## Step 2: non-interactive read
+
+Command (from W00 section 6, verbatim):
+
+```text
+env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin /usr/bin/python3 -I -c "import subprocess; r=subprocess.run(['/usr/bin/security','find-generic-password','-s','dopemux-mcp-probe','-a','probe','-w'],stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL,capture_output=True,timeout=10); print(r.returncode,len(r.stdout.strip()))"
+```
+
+| Context | ACL | Output | Dialog | Who ran it |
+|---|---|---|---|---|
+| Claude desktop terminal pane (GUI session, login keychain unlocked) | default | `0 24` | not reported | operator |
+| Codex shell tool (run twice) | default | `0 24`, `0 24` | not reported | operator |
+| Claude Code Bash tool (this session) | default | `0 24` | none possible: stdin closed, no one to click, returned at once | team lead, 2026-11-01T06:05:15Z, with the operator's approval |
+| No GUI session (`ssh localhost` or a `launchd` agent) | — | NOT_RUN | — | — |
+| Locked login keychain | — | NOT_RUN | — | — |
+| ACL variants `-T ""`, `-T /usr/bin/python3`, `-A` | — | NOT_RUN | — | — |
+
+**Length 24, not 12.** The operator's input doubles every character, as in their chat messages, so the stored value is the 24-character doubled string. The test checks only the exit code and whether a dialog blocks the read, so this does not affect the result.
+
+## Decision for W03 (B01)
+
+- **Item ACL: the default.** The helper creates items with `/usr/bin/security add-generic-password` and no `-T` or `-A`. It reads them with `/usr/bin/security find-generic-password … -w` from a child process.
+  - All three tested runner contexts read without being blocked while the login keychain was unlocked.
+  - `-A` is rejected: it is the widest exposure.
+  - `-T <python>` is rejected: it re-prompts whenever the interpreter path changes.
+- **Untested contexts fail closed.** The locked keychain and the no-GUI session were not run. The helper treats any nonzero exit, timeout or empty output as a hard failure. The message names the likely cause (keychain locked or no login session) and the fix. It never retries into a prompt.
+  - Reads close stdin and use a timeout.
+- **The token never goes on a command line.**
+  - `security add-generic-password -w <token>` would show the token in `ps`.
+  - To write, the helper feeds the command to `security -i` on stdin (W00 section 6), or uses an equivalent that keeps the token out of argv.
+  - Tests stub `security` and assert the token is never in argv, stdout, logs or files.
+- **Same-user exposure is unchanged.** Any process of the same macOS user can run the same read. That is the design's stated non-protection (2A design section 6) and stays so.
+
+## Still to do (operator)
+
+Delete the probe item:
+
+```text
+security delete-generic-password -s dopemux-mcp-probe -a probe
+```
+
+The locked-keychain and no-GUI runs remain optional. They would only change the failure message, not the ACL choice.

+## Residual risks (for J02 and the final audit)
+
+1. **Direct HTTP MCP clients must now supply the exact bound `workspace_id` and an `instance_id` on writes.** The design says the stdio route fills them (rev 2 line 78). It does not say how a client that talks to dope-memory over HTTP directly (Claude Code's `http` entry) learns the bound value. Until W04/W07 answer this, such writes fail with a 400 or a ToolError.
+2. `WriteAuthMiddleware` buffers every `POST /mcp` body in all modes; bodies over 4 MiB get 413 even in `off` and `log`.
+3. `/health` is unauthenticated and now lists caller labels (User-Agent, up to 64 characters, at most 100 callers).
+4. The idempotent `ADD COLUMN` guard in `sqlite_migrations.py` applies to every migration file, not only v1.3.0 (it only makes older migrations more tolerant).
+5. Not covered by W01: `trajectory_state` upserts carry no principal; `capture_client.py` rows stay `unverified` until W06; `wma_mcp/server.py` (red lane, not exempt, not wired to HTTP) does not accept `write_principal`.

+## Open items
+
+1. **Neither rebuilt image would start (operator decision).** Both Dockerfiles copy modules one by one, and neither copies the new `auth_identity.py`:
+   - `docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/Dockerfile:30-38` (W02; imported unguarded by `enhanced_server.py`, `server.py`, `conport_mcp_stdio.py`)
+   - `services/working-memory-assistant/Dockerfile.dope-memory:47-51` (W01, found by the team lead while checking W02's report; imported unguarded by `dope_memory_main.py` and `mcp_http.py`)
+
+   Both Dockerfiles are red-lane paths that ADR-227 does not exempt, and no R2 write surface lists them. The design missed them. Clearing it needs the operator to amend ADR-227 with two more file-exact exemptions and extend a write surface, then one `COPY` line per Dockerfile. Until then, do not rebuild either image from this branch.
+2. **Deploy order.** Id validation applies in every auth mode, so any ConPort or dope-memory writer that omits the ids gets 400 once the new images run. W05 covers the 11 known HTTP writers (the child's report wrongly said W05 covers only the two dopecon-bridge files); W05 B03 lists further writers as blockers before enforce. The images must not deploy before W05 lands.
+3. **Binding variables differ by server.** dope-memory binds to `DOPE_MEMORY_WORKSPACE_ID` (worktree directory name); ConPort binds to `DOPEMUX_WORKSPACE_ID` (`identity.workspace_id or worktree_root`, `lifecycle.py:1221`). A caller that sends one workspace string to both servers may be rejected by one of them. W04 must send each server its own binding value; J02 checks it.
+4. **Direct HTTP MCP clients** (W01 residual 1) now applies to ConPort too: write tools gained required ids, which changes the schemas Claude Code sees.
+5. Smaller notes from the child, recorded for the audit: `_log_progress` now stores the request's instance (it stored NULL before); `_log_decision` now writes `created_by_instance`; `update_context` URL-quotes the workspace id; unverified counts can double for `custom_data` writes through REST `/mcp`; the forwarded-caller header is trusted for counting only when the request authenticated as `internal:bridge-proxy`; `direct_server.py` (legacy, not in the image) still defines unauthenticated write routes.

A hash file written by `dopemux.auth.hashfile` verifies the token as `runner:codex` on both dope-memory's and ConPort's `AuthGate("enforce")`; a wrong token stays `unverified`; `auth_hash_file` is `ok`; directory 0700, file 0600 |
+| `git diff --check`, pre-commit (`SKIP=markdownlint`) | 0 | PASS (markdownlint NOT_RUN) |

+| Real keychain write via `security -i`, exit 44 for a missing item, `-U` update, locked keychain | — | NOT_RUN; first exercised by the operator's `dopemux auth issue <principal> --apply` |
+
+## Helper API for W02, W04 and W05
+
+`dopemux.auth`:
+
+| Kind | Names |
+|---|---|
+| Keychain | `get_token(principal) -> str`, `store_token(principal, token)`, `delete_token(principal) -> bool` |
+| Tokens and principals | `generate_token()`, `hash_token(token)`, `validate_principal(p)`, `is_known_principal(p)` |
+| Constants | `KNOWN_PRINCIPALS`, `SERVICE = "dopemux-mcp"` |
+| Errors | `AuthError`, with subclasses `InvalidPrincipalError`, `KeychainError` (and its subclass `TokenNotFoundError`), `HashFileError` |
+
+`dopemux.auth.hashfile` holds the hash file (`memory-token-hashes.json`) and a date-only sidecar (`memory-token-meta.json`, principal to created/rotated times; no tokens or hashes).
+
+## Notes for the audit
+
+- **Token out of argv.** Writes send `add-generic-password -U -s dopemux-mcp -a <principal> -w <token>` on `security -i` stdin. argv is `["-i"]`. Output is captured and never logged or returned. Success requires the read-back to match (constant-time compare), because `security -i` can exit 0 after a failed command.
+- **Leading dash.** `generate_token` redraws any token that would start with `-`. That excludes 1 in 64 values and costs no meaningful entropy.
+- **Exit 44** as "item not found" comes from macOS `security(1)` behaviour. It has not been observed here: UNKNOWN until a live run.
+- **No transaction.** `issue` and `rotate` write the keychain, then the hash file, then the sidecar. A failure partway leaves a state that re-running the command repairs. A keychain-only token has no hash and verifies nothing.

+## Team-lead clarification given at dispatch
+
+B01 says the bridge reads only `*_PORT` keys from `.envrc.dopemux-mcp`; B04 says the ids come from the envrc. The ruling: read `*_PORT` plus the four id keys, nothing else. The file also exports a database password.
+
+The bridge reads even less: three keys per server, filtered by name before parsing.
+- dope-memory: `DOPE_MEMORY_PORT`, `DOPE_MEMORY_WORKSPACE_ID`, `DOPE_MEMORY_INSTANCE_ID`
+- ConPort: `CONPORT_MCP_PORT`, `DOPEMUX_WORKSPACE_ID`, `DOPEMUX_INSTANCE_ID`
+
+The keys are generated at `lifecycle.py:1219-1233`, and `envrc.py:90-96` lists them as non-secret.
+
+## Rulings on the return
+
+1. **Ids filled on read tools: accepted.** B04 says "mutating calls only". The bridge fills any tool that declares the id, read tools included, but only when the caller omitted it, only with the caller's own repo identity, and it never overrides. That widens nothing and matches dope-memory's read default (the bound workspace). Recorded for the audit.
+2. **Correction: the forwarded `tools/list` no longer marks filled ids as required.** Before it, a model told `workspace_id` was required would invent a value; the server rejects a mismatched workspace, and the bridge never fills a supplied value. Now only the copy printed to the client changes; the bridge's own schema knowledge is untouched, and an id the envrc lacks stays required. This closes W01 residual 1 for runners on the bridge (Codex and Claude Code in 2A). Direct HTTP clients are unaffected.
+

+## What W07 must render
+
+- Invocation: `<python> -m dopemux.mcp.http_stdio_bridge --server dope-memory|conport --principal <principal>`, with an optional `--timeout N`.
+- The process must start inside the checkout: discovery walks up from `Path.cwd()` and has no `--workdir` option. Whether Codex starts stdio servers in the session's working directory is UNKNOWN, so W07 confirms it or sets the server's working directory explicitly.
+- The legacy `--base-url` mode is unchanged. Its only runtime caller is `src/dopemux/config/manager.py:721`.
+
+## Notes for the audit
+
+- **Worktree boundary.** The envrc walk stops at the first directory that has its own `.git` (file or directory) and no envrc, so a worktree under `.worktrees/` never reads the main checkout's file (W00 design change 11).
+- **Exit codes** (the child's choice; the packet did not set them): 2 for a startup failure, 3 for 401/403.
+- **404 retry.** A 404 with a session id triggers one replay of the stored `initialize`; a second 404 is an error.
+- **Token kept on 127.0.0.1.** Redirects are refused and environment proxies are ignored.

+
+How the callers behave:
+- Tokens are fetched on writes only. Without a token, the request goes out exactly as before, and only the principal name is logged.
+- Ids come from the caller, the constructor or the envrc env. A caller's value is never replaced, and no id is invented: a missing id gets the server's 400.
+
+## Open items for the operator (before either server goes to `enforce`)
+
+1. **Tests read the real keychain once tokens are issued.**
+   - Existing caller tests do not stub `get_token`. Today no items exist, so a read finds nothing and the old behaviour continues.
+   - After `dopemux auth issue <principal> --apply` on this Mac, those test runs would read real tokens.
+   - Fakes without a `headers` argument would then also fail.
+   - Fix: make W05's `keychain_guard_plugin` an autouse guard in `tests/conftest.py`. That file is outside every R2 write surface, so it needs operator scope.
+   - This must land before tokens are issued.
+2. **Container callers cannot present a token.** The adhd_engine, claude_brain and dopecon-bridge images have no `/usr/bin/security`, so `get_token` raises and the callers stay `unverified`. This is the same gap as W02's proxy fallback (`PROXY_TOKEN_PROVIDER = lambda: None`). Options:
+   - a host-side proxy;
+   - a token mounted into the container read-only;
+   - moving those writes to the host.
+
+   This is an operator decision.
+3. **Container ids are unknown.**
+   - The compose entries for dopecon-bridge and adhd-engine set no `DOPEMUX_WORKSPACE_ID` or `DOPEMUX_INSTANCE_ID`.
+   - The bridge defaults to a hardcoded `/Users/hue/code/dopemux-mvp` (`config.py:70`).
+   - Once the new images run, those writes get 400 whatever the auth mode. Fixing it is a compose change.
+4. **Bridge progress writes lose `instance_id`.** `ProgressRequest` (`services/dopecon-bridge/.../routes.py:112-122`) has no `instance_id` field. That file is red lane and not exempt.
+5. **Other writers still open:**
+   - `services/task-orchestrator` (non-goal)
+   - `pm/adapters/conport.py`, which works only if `CONPORT_URL` points at the bridge, and sends `workspace_id: "default"`
+   - `services/dope-memory/mcp_stdio_adapter.py`, a stale adapter still listed in `config/runtime_authority_manifest.json`
+   - the bridge's `update_context` PATCH, which ConPort answers with 405
+   - W02's proxy fallback
+6. **Host callers need the envrc env loaded** (for example through direnv). Without it `instance_id` is omitted and the server returns 400.
+7. **Deploy order.** The new images reject writes without ids, so the images must not run before this change.
+8. **No token caching.** Each write spawns `security`, with a 10 s timeout if the keychain is locked.
+9. **Helper code is repeated in each caller** because the write surface had no shared module. `dopemux.auth.bearer_headers(principal)` would remove the copies; that is a later cleanup.

+## Team-lead ruling: lowercase companions rejected
+
+The first round also exempted the lowercase spellings `dockerfile` and `dockerfile.dope-memory`. The reason: Claude Code's guard checks the lowercase reading of every path (`dcp_surface_guard.py:104-113`), so a capitalized exempt name never passes it.
+
+PR Steward's gate (`security_release_gate.py:72-73`) and the scanner (`red_lane_scanner.py:83-84`) match the rules case-sensitively. With the companions, a lowercase path in those two directories would also pass the PR gate on a case-sensitive system. The operator authorized exactly two paths, so the companions were removed in the correction round.
+
+The result:
+- The regex layer exempts exactly the two Dockerfiles.
+- The guard still blocks Claude Code's Edit-tool edits to them. That fails closed and is accepted. A later guard change resolves it.
+- ADR-227 Amendment 1 records this.
+
+## Write surface
+
+9 paths, all inside the W10 write_surface. The Dockerfile diffs are exactly +1 line each:
+- `Dockerfile.dope-memory:48`: `COPY services/working-memory-assistant/auth_identity.py .`
+- ConPort `Dockerfile:32`: `COPY docker/mcp-servers-source/conport/auth_identity.py .`
+
+The order was rules file first, then the Dockerfiles (C12).

+
+## The rule as built
+
+| Server | Omitted `workspace_id` becomes | Omitted `instance_id` becomes |
+|---|---|---|
+| dope-memory | `DOPE_MEMORY_WORKSPACE_ID` | `DOPE_MEMORY_INSTANCE_ID`, else `A` |
+| ConPort | `DOPEMUX_WORKSPACE_ID` | where the table has an instance column: `DOPEMUX_INSTANCE_ID`, else `shared` (NULL) |
+
+Blank values count as omitted. A caller's value is never replaced.
+
+ConPort exceptions:
+- `fork_instance` keeps `target_instance` required.
+- `promote_all` refuses `shared`.
+- `custom_data` has no instance column.
+- `decisions.created_by_instance` takes the server instance (the child's reading of "where the table carries one").
+
+**Kept rejections (each has a named test in `W12`):**
+- a supplied workspace that is not the binding (both servers, REST and MCP);
+- a ConPort path workspace that disagrees with the body or the binding;
+- every write to an unbound server;
+- an entry belonging to another workspace when the workspace is omitted.
+
+**Schemas:** every MCP write tool on all four surfaces marks the ids optional, with a description naming the binding:
+- dope-memory HTTP;
+- ConPort SSE/streamable (`server.py`);
+- ConPort stdio (`conport_mcp_stdio.py`);
+- ConPort REST `/mcp`.
+
+The REST models mark them optional too. When ConPort's `update_context` gets no workspace, it takes the path workspace from the binding; if the server is unbound it refuses and sends nothing.
+
+`identity_required_writes` stays `true`, now meaning "writes are bound to this server's workspace".
+
+## Notes for the audit
+
+- **Docs updated** (how-to, ADR-MCPINT-002 amendment draft, `AGENTS.md`, `.claude/claude.md`). The child left the enforce-blocker items "container ids are unknown" and "bridge progress route drops instance_id" unchanged, as B05 asked. They are weaker now that the servers fill omitted ids.
+- **The stdio tools' parameter order changed** (required arguments first). No code imports or calls them positionally; they are reached only by keyword as MCP tools (`git grep conport_mcp_stdio`).

 import asyncio
 import aiohttp
 import json
+import os
 from typing import Dict, Any, Optional, List
 import logging
 
 logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
 
+# Wave 2A R2 W05: writes carry this principal's token (design 5.3). This service
+# runs in a Linux container, where the macOS keychain does not exist, so
+# get_token fails there and the write goes out unauthenticated (unverified in
+# log mode). Moving a token into the container is a design decision, not made here.
+_PRINCIPAL = "internal:adhd"
+
+
+def _bearer_headers() -> Dict[str, str]:
+    """Authorization header for this caller's principal, or {} when no token is available.
+
+    A missing token never fails a write: in log mode the server records it as
+    unverified. Only the principal name is logged, never a token.
+    """
+    try:
+        from dopemux.auth import AuthError, get_token
+    except ImportError:
+        logger.info("dopemux.auth is unavailable; sending %s writes without a token", _PRINCIPAL)
+        return {}
+    try:
+        return {"Authorization": f"Bearer {get_token(_PRINCIPAL)}"}
+    except AuthError:
+        logger.info("no token for %s; sending the write without Authorization", _PRINCIPAL)
+        return {}
+

+# Wave 2A R2 W05: ConPort writes carry this principal's token (design 5.3). The
+# bridge runs in a Linux container (compose.yml dopecon-bridge, Dockerfile), where
+# the macOS keychain does not exist, so get_token fails there and the write goes
+# out unauthenticated (unverified in log mode). Moving a token into the container
+# is a design decision, not made here.
+_PRINCIPAL = "internal:dopecon"
+_WRITE_METHODS = frozenset({"POST", "PUT", "PATCH", "DELETE"})
+
+
+def _bearer_headers() -> Dict[str, str]:
+    """Authorization header for this caller's principal, or {} when no token is available.
+
+    A missing token never fails a write: in log mode the server records it as
+    unverified. Only the principal name is logged, never a token.
+    """
+    try:

+    except ImportError:

+        return {}

+        return {"Authorization": f"Bearer {get_token(_PRINCIPAL)}"}
+    except AuthError:
